AI資本支出持續攀升，現金流承壓加劇企業信用分化
• AI基礎建設投資擴大，企業融資需求同步升溫：AI競賽已由
模型及技術開發延伸至資料中心、運算晶片與電力基礎設施
建置，帶動大型科技企業持續提高資本支出。Microsoft、
Alphabet、Amazon等企業近期公布的財報均顯示，AI相關
投資已進入高強度建置階段，市場預估大型科技企業2026年
AI基礎建設投資將超過7,000億美元，龐大資金需求也使市場
更加關注企業融資成本、資本配置效率及未來現金流表現。
• 財報反映投資成本，現金流與信用評等成市場檢驗重點：Q3
財報顯示，Alphabet及Amazon均因AI基礎建設投資大幅增
加，使自由現金流承受壓力；其中Amazon近12個月自由現
金流轉為負值，Alphabet單季亦出現負自由現金流。相較之

下，Microsoft仍維持正向自由現金流，反映企業財務承受能
力有所差異。此外，Meta因AI資本支出規模擴大及未來現金
流不確定性，遭標普全球調降信用評等展望至負向，顯示市
場已開始將AI投資效益納入企業信用風險評估。
• 市場看法：AI長期需求仍具成長潛力，但市場評價將逐步由
資本支出規模轉向投資報酬率、自由現金流及財務槓桿管理。
而具備穩定營運現金流、較高資本回收效率及明確AI商業化
成果的企業，較有能力維持投資動能；反之，若資本支出持
續攀升而營收及獲利未能同步跟上，將增加企業融資需求與
信用風險，並使科技企業間的評價差異進一步擴大。
大型科技企業擴大資本支出，承諾規模持續攀升
AI產業表外承諾大幅揚升
資料來源：Morgan Stanley Research，企業10-Q、10-K、8-K申報文件，ZeroHedge，
兆豐銀行彙整。

A股短期反彈行情可望延續，長期聚焦科技成長
• A股短期反彈行情可望延續，長期聚焦科技成長：從A股內部條件來看，
市場經歷長時間修正後，估值與情緒指標已降至歷史低檔，創業板本
益比回落至約26.5%，全市場站上200日均線個股占比僅約一成，顯示
悲觀情緒已相當充分反映，搭配市場成交熱度回升，盤面觀察近期銀
行、消費等權值板塊逐漸止穩，科技成長類股也開始出現籌碼與評價
修復，顯示市場輪動條件逐步成熟。短期而言，A股反彈行情仍有機會
延續，主因為美國期中選舉落幕、中國政策環境偏寬鬆，以及A股估值
與情緒指標落在歷史相對低檔，具備超跌反彈條件，政策面方面，人
行可能進一步降息，政策利率由1.25%下調至1%，搭配IPO超募資金
回購、穩定資金加大ETF買入力道，有助於改善市場流動性並提振投資

信心。
• 投資建議：操作上可優先布局半導體、AI硬體、通訊設備、電子零組
件、醫藥及機器人等具成長題材的族群，並留意商業航太等新興應用，
不過，反彈過程仍須觀察政策落實程度、企業獲利修復及海外市場變
化，中長期而言，A股仍以科技創新與科技服務實體經濟為核心主軸，
相關政策方向並未改變，建議維持科技成長為核心配置，搭配具景氣
復甦潛力的週期與消費產業，採取分批布局。
各指數估值與情緒指標已降至歷史低檔
科技產業獲利可望提升

中國10月製造業PMI跌回收縮區間，產需動能同步降溫
• 中國10月製造業PMI跌回收縮區間，產需動能同步降溫：中國10月製造業PMI降至49.3%，低於市場預期，也跌破50榮枯線，
顯示製造業景氣再度轉弱，主要受到十一長假前需求提前釋放，以及前期海外聖誕訂單集中出貨後進入調整期影響，使需求與
生產動能同步放緩，新訂單指數和生產指數皆下降，顯示企業生產活動亦有所收斂，不過若與去年同期相比，新訂單與生產指
數分別增加0.6及0.1個百分點，顯示製造業基本面仍具一定韌性，景氣表現優於去年同期，此外企業對未來仍維持審慎樂觀態
度，生產經營活動預期指數為53.4%，雖較前月略降，但仍位於擴張區間。
• 展望後市：隨著專項債加速投放，及8,000億元政策性金融工具逐步轉化為實體投資，基建及製造業需求有望持續釋放，帶動裝

備製造、鋼鐵等產業鏈景氣改善，此外11月為企業衝刺全年營運目標的重要時點，訂單接單與生產活動將較10月回溫，產業表
現方面，科技業因受惠於政策扶持及產業升級趨勢，維持強勁動能，消費品製造業則有望受雙十一購物帶動成長。

民主黨主張監管政策 間接支撐黃金價格表現
• 期中選舉為黃金價格帶來短期支撐：民主黨主導國會加深兩黨政治僵局的擔憂，加上可能推動更嚴格的AI產業監管，市場擔
憂對美國經濟成長帶來負面影響，進而增加聯準會採取寬鬆貨幣政策的可能性，同時美元走貶也是上週支撐金價的因素之一。
• 央行需求基本面穩健：世界黃金協會最新報告指出，各國央行在9月持續增加黃金持有量，當月淨購買量達61噸，為今年迄
今最高的單月總量。同時數據顯示，全球各國央行所申報的黃金淨購買量為228噸，較去年同期下降 7%。
• 觀點：央行購金需求維持高檔，但相較去年同期累計未進一步放大，同時高水準的美債殖利率排擠黃金的避險需求。在此情
境下，短中期黃金價格能否進一步突破的關鍵，可能取決於聯準會貨幣政策的調整空間。雖然市場大致排除激進升息的預期，

但緩步升息仍是基本情境，預料近期黃金價格將在每盎司4,000~4,200震盪。消息面來看，美伊衝突維持僵局，不過若是荷
姆茲海峽議題能有重大突破，將為黃金帶來進一步上漲空間。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，資料統計至9月底，2026.11.4。
金價短線技術面壓力在4,200美元(週線)
資料來源：Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理，2026.11.6。
央行購金量為黃金帶來基本面支撐

期中選舉牽動，上週國際油價大幅震盪
• 選舉結果令國際油價大跌：選舉結果出爐後，市場預期民主黨將在預算與軍事決策上牽制總統川普，促使中東僵局加速透過
外交協商落幕。結果公布當天布蘭特油價單日大跌約9%，至每桶94美元，臨近週末則小幅反彈。上週油價整體收跌約16%，
主要反映選舉風險溢價消退，不過美伊僵局未變，油價仍在100美元附近震盪。
• 中東原油出口接近戰前水準，但成品油出口仍大幅落後：波斯灣國家正致力原油出口替代方案，目標擺脫荷姆茲海峽航運依
賴。石油出口量近幾週雖有所回落，但已接近戰前平均水準；然而，成品油出口動能恢復緩慢，目前仍遠不及戰前平均，這
也是造成全球能源通膨的主要原因。
• 觀點：波斯灣國家能源出口替代方案已略見成效，不過由於替代路徑運輸成本較高，能源價格仍居高不下，觀察過去幾週出

口及價格變化，推測在美伊關係未有明確變化的情境下，國際油價將在每桶100美元附近震盪。

新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，

 投資評價與建議 
維持買進，目標價降至 358 元：(1) 3Q26 獲利表現不如預期，主因 PCB 比重拉升
及面對中國在地價格競爭，(2) 能見度高，但隨企業 AI Server 帶動 HDI/HLC PCB 
與 ABF 載板 2H26-2027 年接力放量，短期獲利改善有限。我們預估 2026/ 2027
年 EPS 為 14.01/19.92 元，目標 PE 則由 30x 下修至 18x，反映市場對設備類股評
價的調整，目標價隨之下修到 358 元(18x 2027 EPSF)。

### Page 2

2

志聖以光與熱為核心，發展熱處理平台：志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製
程等技術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備
起家，而後伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、面板、太陽能等產業，近
年陸續在 IC 載板、高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值市場發光發熱。公司
也與均豪、均華、東捷組成 G2C+聯盟，共同實現台灣先進封裝在地化。志聖主要
生產據點位於台灣林口、台中、中國廣州、昆山，並在台南、廣東、泰國、美國鳳
凰城有服務據點。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、
傳統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。主要競爭對手包含 EVG、TOK、SUSS、
Yield Engineering Systems(YES)等。客戶包含台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等電子中上游廠商。 
3Q26 獲利不如預期：志聖 3Q26 營收 29.8 億(+7.0%QoQ，+93.9%YoY)，毛利
率 43.3%，稅後淨利 5.5 億(-13.4%QoQ，+162.1%YoY)，EPS 3.51 元。毛利率
表現不如預期主因出貨較多中國 PCB 用低階設備，使產品組合轉差，即半導體設
備/PCB 設備由 1H26 的 46%/46%變動至 1-3Q26 的 45%/49%，營業費用亦隨
獎酬增加及研發專案數的提升而高於預期。 
能見度高，但我們對獲利趨於審慎：管理層預期 2026 年底在手訂單約 100-110
億，較去年同期約 43 億元倍增，其中半導體佔比約 53%以上(以 OSAT 能見度較
高)、高階 PCB 35-37%。值得注意的是，半導體設備交期已由 4-10 個月拉長至 4-
13 個月；PCB 設備交期則約 3-8 個月，主因 OSAT 在廠房未完工前即下單，以及
上游零組件(機械手臂)交期的拉長所致，志聖持續以 G2C+聯盟和日系供應商溝通
並聯合採購，目前風險可控。展望後市，我們預期在晶圓廠先進封裝產能建置外溢
的環境下，志聖將透過挑單生產，降低傳統 PCB 接單比例以維持獲利率穩健成長。 
載板挖槽嵌入製程，2028 年再添動能：隨 AI 晶片尺寸持續放大，台系載板廠正
發展新的生產方式，以降低整片翹曲同時達到微縮線寬/線距的目的。志聖在此製
程中提供背面貼膜，以及 ABF 絕緣膜的壓合與撕膜設備。由於層數倍增將使壓合
與撕膜次數同步增加，加上載具尺寸放大推升單機價值，我們認為這將是志聖 
2028 年後載板設備的重要成長來源。 
財務預估：研究部預估志聖 2026 年營收 111.1 億元(+82.2%YoY)，毛利率 46.3%，
稅後淨利 22.7 億元(+173.0%YoY)，EPS 14.01 元(原估 16.94 元，下修主因本業
獲利表現不如預期及股本膨脹)。展望 2027 年，公司尚未給予明確展望，我們考量
短期 PCB/HLC 急單湧現壓抑毛利率，故下修獲利預估，預期 2027 年營收 148.4
億元(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 46.9%，稅後淨利 32.2 億元(+42.2%YoY)，EPS 19.92
元(原估 25.15 元，下修主因獲利預估調降及股本膨脹)。

## Document 109: 261117_daiwa_merida

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_daiwa_merida.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Merida hosted its 3Q26 investor call on 17 November after market hours, 
following release of its 3Q26 results on 11 November (see our memo). Merida’s gross 
margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our estimate 
(16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Factoring in the equity investment loss of 
TWD150m+, its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the 
consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results recap. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales, the price discount 
for further inventory digestion and the depreciation of the Euro. Factoring in the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus estimates by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was 
TWD1.06, lower than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).  
• 2026 outlook update. Merida revised down its 2026 full-year shipments to 960k units 
(Taiwan plant 280k, down from previous 340k; China plant 680k, down from previous 
700k), as it continues to manage its existing model shipments and expects new models 
in 2027 to bring stronger demand. The company now sees recovery in Europe and 
China, with inventory digestion coming to an end: 9M26 sales in Europe grew 14% YoY, 
driven by strong demand for road bikes and gravel bikes; 9M 26 sales in China grew by 
1% while shipments grew by 7% (vs. revenue guidance of declining 15-20% at the 
beginning of 2026), driven by low- and mid-end models. 
• SBC expected to be profitable in 4Q26. Due to SBC’s conservative tax assumption 
(over 100% effective tax rate) in 1Q-3Q26, if and when the tax rate realised at year-end 
is determined to be less than currently allocated for, we expect there will be a huge profit 
recognised in 4Q26 to offset the estimated after-tax loss recognised in 1Q-3Q26. Merida 
and SBC will review the tax assumption policy after full-year results are out, as the 
policy has caused a great deal of investor confusion. Including USD15m of tariff refund 
in 3Q26, SBC’s pre-tax profit grew by 42% YoY for 9M26. Merida expects there is 
another USD5m of tariff refund to collect in 4Q26.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 
13.2x/10.6x, based on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-5-year one-year-forward PER 
trading range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the 
EU and the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory 
digestion.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

assist with process execution, which we think should shorten the learning curve. Nevertheless, depreciation is
expected to rise sharply from approximately NT$9.1bn in 2026 to NT$14–16bn in 2027. As a result, consolidated GM
could fall from above 32% in 2026 to roughly 25–30% in 2027, with gradual improvement throughout 2027 as
utilization improves. The established 8-inch business could reach the mid-30% range, while the new 12-inch may

remain at a 20–30% GM during its depreciation period.
• Market turbulence, recovery path and risks. VIS believes AI-related power-management demand remains intact
despite concerns about offshore computing restrictions for Chinese AI, project financing and possible changes in
GPU deployment. Power content rises with increasing compute density, faster signal transitions and more complex
voltage-management requirements across GPUs, ASICs, networking and cooling systems. For traditional consumer

electronics market, where high memory prices are raising notebook and smartphone costs and delaying replacement
demand. VIS has partly protected itself by cutting consumer capacity by more than 20% and reallocating it to AI and
server products. Other risks include higher substrate, chemical, gas, electricity and labor costs, as well as slower
Singapore qualification. Longer term GM should recover toward or above 30% in 2028–29 if the new fab reaches

targeted utilization, but the pace will depend on interposer volumes, IC yields and customer ramp schedules.

Valuation
Our target price for VIS of NT$160 is based on 20x 2027E P/E, which we see as justified by its robust PMIC sales growth
momentum and increasing outflow order opportunity from TSMC with support from its consistent cash dividend policy. Our
target price equates to 2026/27E P/B of 3.8x/3.7x.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price include: 1) weaker 8-inch demand along with higher costs due to inflation or more severe

ASP competition from Chinese peers; 2) lower UTR of the new capacity at the newly acquired fab, with depreciation burden;
and 3) less-than-expected outsourcing opportunities from IDMs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

高階 ABF 展望維持正向  
◼ 3Q26 本業獲利符合預期，單季稅後 EPS 6.36 元：3Q26 營收
526.50 億元，+23%QOQ，+55%YOY，+38%累計 YOY；毛利率
28.85%，營益率 20%，本業獲利表現符合預期，業外收入 26.36 億
元，稅前淨利 131.63 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 100.52 億元，
3Q26 單季稅後 EPS 6.36 元。 
◼ 高階 ABF 展望維持正向：展望後市，2027 年客戶產品 Roadmap 及
產能配置大致底定，且多已支付預付款，即便個別客戶需求出現變
化，亦可透過產能重新分配因應，整體高階 ABF 載板仍供不應求。
目前 AI 相關營收佔比達 70%，高階 ABF 受惠 AI 晶片尺寸及層數提
升，合理毛利率可達 50~60%。產能方面，光復二廠產能已全數獲
客戶預訂，欣興亦透過既有 BT 產線改造以緩解 ABF Core 產能瓶
頸，預估 2027~2028 年 ABF 產能每年增幅皆可達 20~30%。客戶透
過 LTA 及預付款方式確保長期高階產能，預期欣興未來幾年仍將受
惠高階產品佔比提升及產品組合優化。  
◼ 維持買進評等：預估 2026 年 EPS 為 23.49 元；隨生產工藝逐步提
升，欣興高階 ABF 產品合理毛利率為 50~60%，推估 2027 年 EPS
為 45.17 元，維持買進，目標價調整至 900 元(20x2027EPS)。

欣興為全球 IC 載板領導廠商  欣興電子為全球印刷電路板與 IC 載板製造領導廠商，屬於聯電旗下，總部位
於桃園龜山；產品涵蓋 ABF 載板、BT 載板、高密度連接板(HDI)、多層板、
FPC 及軟硬結合板等，廣泛應用於 AI 伺服器、高效能運算、網通設備、智慧
型手機、 消費性電子及車用電子等領域。目前生產基地位於台灣、中國、泰
國等地區， ABF/BT 載板、HDI 主要在台灣、中國生產，其中高階 ABF 廠區位
於新竹光復、 桃園楊梅，至於 PCB 主要在泰國進行生產。

2025 年產品營收佔比：Substrate 58%(ABF 46%+BT 12%)、HDI 27%、PCB 
11%、FPC 2%、其他 1%。預估 2026 年 Substrate 佔比 63%，+58%YOY， 
2026-2027 年成長以 ABF 為主，預期 2026 年 ABF 載板+74%YOY，BT 載板-
3%YOY，HDI 佔比 25%、PCB 佔比 9%、FPC 佔比 1%、其他佔比 1%。

高階 ABF 展望維持正向  展望後市，2027 年客戶產品 Roadmap 及產能配置大致底定，且多已支付預付

載板仍供不應求。目前 AI 相關營收佔比達 70%，高階 ABF 受惠 AI 晶片尺寸
及層數提升，合理毛利率可達 50~60%。1) ABF：除既有 CoWoS 需求持續強
勁之外，EMIB-T 亦獲 Google TPU 以外的終端客戶關注，考量新平台開發將
排擠既有 CoWoS 相關載板產能，載板供應商 EMIB-T 產品毛利率要求更高，
預期未來設備配置將取決於 3 家載板廠(IBIDEN、Shinko、欣興)的良率及量產
能力；2)HDI: 3Q26 AI HDI 佔 HDI 約 50%、光模組佔約 30%，其餘 20%為其
他產品。

「逢低買進」，目標價: $3,300

報告日期：2026.11.16 研究員：陳泂暉

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/13 收盤價： 1,520.00 毛利重返成長軌道，上調投資評等至「買進」 
目前股價 12X27(F)PER，考量： 
(1) 26Q3 公 司 營 收 為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6% 、
YoY+51.3%，受惠 HPC 電源需求、SPE 沉積蝕刻及次
系統提升與電氣化推進；毛利率為 32.6% ，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，費用率創低帶動營益率創
高；稅後淨利 42.78億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；
稅後 EPS 21.93元。 
(2) 工業業務受惠工廠自動化回溫及能源、電動車等電氣化
需求推進，機器人、航太與醫療等客製化方案亦逐步貢
獻。半導體設備受惠沉積與蝕刻滲透率提升，客戶投資
看至 2030年後，帶動子系統整合與機箱組裝委外需求擴
大。 
(3) HPC受惠 AI基礎設施升級，機櫃內銅纜與機櫃外光纖將
長期共存。公司透過 XFS 強化光學封裝與量產，推進
CPO、12.8T XPO 及 Micro-VCSEL 技術；電源延伸至
Busbars、匯流排槽及水冷板等全液冷機櫃方案，結合
Interplex 機構整合並共同開發 LVDC/HVDC 架構，推升
系統級競爭力。 
(4) 預估 2027 年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於
新世代平台放量；毛利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於
產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，YoY+74.6%；稅
後 EPS 131.58元。

貿聯目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，位於公司過往 9-34X區間
下緣位置，考量公司持續推進光通業務，將有助於評價回升，
所以取 17X，給予「買進」投資評等， 6 個月目標價
2,200(17X2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 44.74%。

資料來源：CMoney；兆豐國際預估

訪談重點

全球連接線束解決方案領導者 
貿聯成立於 1996 年，總部位於加州矽谷，註冊於開曼群島，為全球連接線
束解決方案的領導廠商。產品分為工業用、電器用、車用、資訊科技與其他
五大產線。主要應用於資訊科技用線、電腦週邊設備、數據中心用線、消費
電子用線、電器用線、車用線、醫療設備用線、光通訊設備、太陽能連接裝
置、工業設備用線、半導體設備等線材、連接器等領域。公司 26Q1 產品營
收比重為: 工業用 31%、電器用 12%、車用 8%、資訊科技 48%。

HPC 與半導體設備雙引擎驅動營益率創歷史新高 
26Q3 公司營收為 278.54 億元，QoQ+19.6%、YoY+51.3%，細分產品組合
來看，HPC 高效能運算受惠於 AI 基礎設施持續投資，其中電源互連因高電
流與機櫃級配電需求強勁、旗下 XFS光學業務月營收運行率自年初以來已超
過翻倍、SPE半導體生產設備受惠於沉積與蝕刻等設備平台滲透率及次系統
承接比重提升，並持續大幅優於整體 WFE 市場、其他業務整體市場環境好
壞參半，但電氣化與自動化需求持續推進，另併購之 Interplex於 26Q3 僅計
入 7天營收，10月起完整併表並單月貢獻 6,700萬美元；毛利率為 32.6%，
QoQ+1.9ppts、YoY-0.7ppts，搭配營業費用率降至歷史新低，帶動營業利益
率創下歷史新高；稅後淨利 42.78 億元，QoQ+43.5%、YoY+62.8%；稅後
EPS 21.93元。

動能從單一零組件邁向子系統級解決方案，動能延續至 2027年後 
工業用業務方面，受惠工廠自動化需求活動持續改善，加上受惠於電氣化與
基礎設施現代化的能源、電動車及特用工程應用穩健推進，帶動整體表現持
續成長；此外，公司在機器人、航太、海事、醫療科技、半導體量測及儲能
等領域的客製化線束與整合方案，部分已成為穩定貢獻來源，部分則處於早
期認證與商業開發階段。

半導體生產設備方面，主要受惠於沉積與蝕刻設備平台滲透率與內含價值提
升，且客戶針對高複雜度製程的投資預期強勁並延伸至 2030 年以後，帶動
高複雜度組裝、子系統製造與整合、測試及機箱組裝的委外需求持續擴大。
隨著各產業客戶對供應商的要求從單一零組件轉向具備工程支援、產品認

- 3 - 
訪談速報 
證、量產準備度與穩定執行力的子系統級解決方案，結合併購 Interplex所帶
來的精密機構與機殼整合綜效，相關成長動能預期將延續至 2027 年及更長
遠的基礎設施與產能擴張週期。

機櫃內銅纜、機櫃外光纖並進，提供完整互連方案 
公司持續受惠於高速銅互連解決方案在支援更高頻寬與維持訊號完整性上的
優勢；隨著 AI系統頻寬需求、光纖密度與互連複雜度提高，光互連基礎設施
的重要性持續提升，同時銅互連在機櫃內短距離傳輸、成本與部署特性上仍
具效率優勢，因此光互連不會單向取代銅互連，而是機櫃內以銅纜為主、機
櫃外長距離傳輸以光纖為主，兩者將依頻寬、傳輸距離、功耗與封裝架構不
同而長期共存。公司透過旗下 XFS Communications 補強精密光學組裝、連
接器化製程、高密度封裝與快速量產擴張能力，並陸續於 OFC、Computex
與 OCP展出光纖方案、CPO交換器托盤及 12.8T XPO收發方案，同時推進
次世代基於 Micro-VCSEL的機櫃內擴展（Scale-up）光學互連技術與外部技
術合作，成為後續 HPC業務擴張的重要動能。

在 HPC端，AI基礎設施進入長期投資與升級週期，隨著算力密度持續攀升，
資料中心的物理基礎設施挑戰正延伸至高電流電力傳輸、散熱及系統級整
合。公司產品線已從單一電源線延伸至 Busbars、Power Whips、連接器、
匯流排槽以及水冷板、分歧管、液冷快接頭與漏液偵測等全液冷 AI 機櫃方
案，並結合併購 Interplex的精密機構與機殼整合能力強化系統級競爭力；至
於低壓直流與高壓直流新電源架構的導入管理層指出將取決於各家客戶的系
統設計、產品認證進度及資料中心電力基礎設施的就緒程度，目前公司正與
客戶密切共同開發次世代與下下世代架構，以高度彈性配合其部署排程。

新世代平台放量帶動獲利爆發 
預估 26Q4營收 339.62億元，QoQ+21.9%、YoY+71.7%，因為工業用季節
性需求回溫，以及 HPC 新平台所帶動；毛利率 32.8%，QoQ+0.2ppts、
YoY+0.9ppts，因新平台導入，因此壓抑毛利率成長幅度；稅後淨利 51.7億
元，QoQ+20.9%、YoY+88.7%；稅後 EPS 26.5元。

預估 2026年營收 1,059.61億元，YoY+48.7%，持續受惠於資料中心業務；
毛利率 31.5%，YoY-0.2ppts；稅後淨利 147.04 億元，YoY+63.3%；稅後
EPS 75.37元。

預估 2027年營收 1,636.24 億元，YoY+54.4%，受惠於新世代平台放量；毛
利率 33.6%，YoY+2.1ppts，受惠於產品組合改善；稅後淨利 256.69 億元，
YoY+74.6%；稅後 EPS 131.58元。

貿聯目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，位於公司過往 9-34X區間下緣位置，考

## Document 224: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

### Page 2

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │3 
26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

## Document 271: Anthropic周五收涨10.98%至91.88美元，重新站上发行价

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic周五收涨10.98%至91.88美元，重新站上发行价.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic周五反弹扭转全周跌势，本周收涨3.34%。公司上市以来股价⼀度⼤跌，部分投
资者质疑，为何允许承销银⾏在上市⾸⽇之后就⾏使超额配售选择权，⽽不是保留这项能
⼒⽤于⽀撑股价。同时投资者还关注解锁问题，最多3.03亿股内部⼈持股预计将于12⽉15
⽇可出售。
摘要：
Anthropic PBC在纳斯达克完成⾸个完整交易周，周五股价收于每股91.88美元，重回83美元的
发⾏价上⽅。
此前，这家由Dario Amodei领导的⼈⼯智能实验室完成了创纪录的⾸次公开募股，规模达1195
亿美元。
周五该股上涨近11%，创下⾃11⽉19⽇上市以来的最⾼收盘价，结束了连续两个交易⽇收盘价
低于发⾏价的⾛势。
当⽇美股因感恩节假期提前收盘，随着⻛险偏好回升，Anthropic跟随⼤盘反弹。
按周五收盘价计算，公司完全稀释市值约为1.7万亿美元，低于上市⾸⽇盘中触及的逾2万亿美元
峰值，当天股价最⾼触及115.10美元，随后回落。
⾸周⾛势反复，⻩仁勋回归、AI投资回报担忧等冲击股价
在Anthropic进⾏史上规模最⼤的IPO之前，市场对⼈⼯智能⽀出节奏的担忧已经冲击科技股。

这⼀情绪在英伟达CEO⻩仁勋回归这家芯⽚公司后进⼀步扩散，投资者开始评估相关争议对AI
⽣态系统的潜在影响。担忧情绪随后蔓延⾄Anthropic股价。
上市⾸周⾛势反复，使此次IPO的执⾏情况受到审视。部分投资者质疑，为何允许承销银⾏在上
市⾸⽇之后就⾏使超额配售选择权，⽽不是保留这项能⼒⽤于⽀撑股价。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

另⼀些投资者则注意到，上市⾸⽇即有最多3.15亿股内部⼈持股解除锁定。根据招股书，这是公
司为期⼀年的分批解禁安排的⼀部分。
招股书显⽰，另⼀批最多3.03亿股内部⼈持股预计将于12⽉15⽇可出售。同样在12⽉，包括纳
斯达克100在内的指数可能纳⼊Anthropic股票，这将带来跟踪基⾦的买⼊潮。
截⾄10⽉底，公司收⼊年化运⾏率为810亿美元。这⼀增⻓势头帮助投资者忽略了公司截⾄
2045年接近9010亿美元的承诺⽀出。

Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event

and Development Authority of India)

3 
incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

impede both the absorption and creation of knowledge, as well as its practical application. Although the

restrictions on talent exchanges and overseas travel that reflect excessive emphasis on technology 
protection could slow China's TFP growth over the medium to long term and act as a headwind to the 
Chinese economy by lowering its potential growth rate. 
(Hiromu Komiya)

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +9.57 điểm tương ứng +0.55%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Tập đoàn công nghiệp, Tiện ích Điện và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng 
rõ rệt nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với 
giá trị -810 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và STB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -13 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -1,161 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -12,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -2,033 
hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,103 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần hồi phục nhẹ với biến động tăng/giảm đan xen, thanh khoản 
ở mức thấp và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài tiếp tục đẩy mạnh bán ròng. Hai phiên giữa tuần 
chứng kiến sự hồi phục mạnh mẽ của thị trường, phản ứng trước thông tin NHNN có động 
thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản hệ thống ngân hàng để hạ lãi suất thông qua nghiệp vụ hoán đổi 
ngoại tệ, và mua vào dự trữ ngoại hối. Dù vậy, áp lực bán nhanh chóng quay trở lại ở 2 
phiên cuối tuần khi nhà đầu tư hoài nghi về triển vọng hạ lãi suất trong ngắn hạn, trong 
khi TTCK toàn cầu cũng có diễn biến kém khả quan trước đà hồi phục của giá dầu, số liệu 
CPI Mỹ cao hơn kỳ vọng, và diễn biến tiêu cực ở nhóm cổ phiếu AI.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành thêm một mẫu nến spinning trên khung tuần, hàm ý tương 
quan cung cầu đang tạm thời cân bằng; trong khi diễn biến tăng nhẹ về điểm số nhưng đi 
kèm sự sụt giảm của thanh khoản cho thấy tín hiệu hồi phục mang tính phân hóa rõ nét. 
Trên khung ngày, nhịp bật tăng tích cực của chỉ số sau khi chạm đường biên dưới của mẫu 
hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn đang cho dấu hiệu chững lại tại vùng kháng cự gần 
quanh 1.750, phản ánh lực cầu mặc dù được củng cố khá tốt ở vùng giá thấp nhưng thiếu 
sự đồng thuận khớp lên các vùng giá cao. Diễn biến giằng co có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy 
nhiên khi xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều tăng, chỉ số được kỳ vọng sẽ tiếp 
tục hướng lên, thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên. 
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports.1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026,2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

A) Higher 10-year Yield Consistent with Strong Nominal Growth Environment. B) Domestic Private Investors Now Dominate U.S. Treasury Market.

Investment Implications
In our view, higher nominal and real
yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can
provide cushion in the face of higher
rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

MARKETS IN REVIEW

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH 
EARNINGS AGGREGATES 
STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                       November 26, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 25.4% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 10.5%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 12.4% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 7.4%.

 294 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 56.8% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 351 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 63.5% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 30, one companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through November 26, 294 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 56.8% 
reported earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (since 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 
39% miss estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 3.7% above estimates, which is below the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting revenues that are 0.7% below estimates, which is below the 1.3% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 25.4%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €165.6B in Q3 2026, compared to share-weighted earnings of €132B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, while the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (150.4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €35.2B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-70.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

The Consumer Cyclicals sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-0.3%) of any sector. It is expected to generate 
sales of €198.6B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €199.2B in Q3 2025.

## Document 389: Thailand Chartbook_ K-shaped divergence (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Thailand Chartbook_ K-shaped divergence (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Chua Han Teng, CFA                        
Senior Economist 
hantengchua@dbs.com

Violet Lee +65 68785281 
Please direct distribution 
queries to  
violetleeyh@dbs.com

ECONOMICS & STRATEGY 
Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence 
17 November 2026 
go.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro/home

groupresc@dbs.com 
• The uptick in Thai GDP growth to 2.9% yoy in 3Q26 from 
2.4% yoy in 2Q masked a widening K-shaped divergence 
in growth. 
• The strong technology-led investment and export cycle 
will continue at a sustainable pace in 2027. 
• Private consumption will stay constrained by high debt, 
a weak labour market, and muted sentiment. 
• Fiscal space to sustain repeated stimulus measures and 
support household spending is narrowing.  
• Soft tourism is set for a seasonal recovery in 4Q26 and 
a gradual improvement in 2027. 
• We expect the BOT to remain on an extended pause 
throughout 2027, supporting an uneven economy. 
• Forecast implications: We expect real GDP growth and 
headline inflation to ease to 2.0% and 1.0%, 
respectively, in 2027, from revised forecasts of 2.4% 
and 2.1% in 2026 (2.1% and 1.7% previously).

### Page 2

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
2

Real GDP growth picked up to 2.9% 
yoy in 3Q26 from an upwardly revised 
2.4% in 2Q. However, the K-shaped 
divergence in growth widened. We 
forecast growth to ease to 2.0% in 
2027 from an adjusted 2.4% in 2026.

Net trade contributed positively in 3Q, 
as export expansion outpaced robust 
import growth, driven by 
supercharged demand for artificial 
intelligence (AI)-related electronics.

Overall growth gained momentum in 
3Q, but domestic demand was highly 
uneven. Investment surged, while 
private and government consumption 
were sluggish, although household 
spending edged up.

Higher GDP growth in 3Q was notably 
driven by stronger technology-
related industrial expansion, while 
the agriculture and services 
sustained their yoy growth.

REAL GDP GROWTH

### Page 3

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
3

Overall investment growth 
accelerated to 11.8% yoy in 3Q26, the 
strongest pace since 4Q12, with the 
private sector set to remain the 
primary driver.

Monthly data showed a sizeable jump 
in machinery and equipment 
investment in September. While this 
increase is unlikely to be sustained, 
we expect it to stay robust given the 
strong investment project pipeline.

Surging investment has been led 
notably by machinery and equipment 
outlays, underpinned by rising capital 
goods imports and local machinery 
sales relating to electronics.

Strong investment applications in the 
first three quarters of 2026 were 
driven mainly by the digital sector, 
which doubled from a year earlier, 
supported by favourable policies, and 
robust foreign investor interest.

INVESTMENT

### Page 4

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
4

Electronics exports momentum 
across key ASEAN economies is 
strong heading into 4Q26 and early 
2027, supported by global AI 
tailwinds and the AI infrastructure 
build-out by major hyperscalers.

The US remains a major driver of 
Thailand’s strong goods export 
performance, with exports growing 
for the 27th consecutive month as of 
September 2026.

Thai telecommunications equipment 
exports are benefiting from the 
global AI boom, alongside other 
growing tech shipments. Electronics 
exports expansion should continue at 
a sustainable rate in 2027.

Thailand’s business sentiment 
remains positive, primarily supported 
by strong order books amid robust 
external demand, despite challenges 
including rising costs.

GOODS EXPORTS

### Page 5

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
5

Private consumption remains 
sluggish, reflecting structural 
constraints from elevated household 
debt and a weak labour market, while 
any improvements has been 
temporarily boosted by stimulus.

Foreign tourism has faced a difficult 
2026, but should recover gradually 
into the seasonally strong year-end. 
Visitor arrivals will improve in 2027 as 
airfares fall alongside policy support.

The uptick in consumer confidence 
has stalled, as households contend 
with high living costs, uneven 
economic growth, and flooding. 
Fiscal space for continued 
government support is narrowing.

Visitor arrivals from most markets fell 
in the first 10 months of 2026, with 
ASEAN recording the weakest 
performance. While Chinese visitors 
rose, the recovery remains weak.

PRIVATE CONSUMPTION AND TOURISM

### Page 6

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
6

Global food and oil prices remain 
above year-ago levels, amid supply-
side shocks from adverse El Niño 
weather conditions, and the 
unresolved conflict in the Middle East.

Headline inflation surged to 3.9% yoy 
in October 2026, the highest in 45 
months, driven by faster energy and 
food price increases. Above-target 
inflation will persist into early 2027 
before supply-side pressures fade.

Thailand’s upstream producer prices 
are facing substantial upward 
pressures, which is feeding through 
to consumer prices, but the extent is 
limited by muted consumer demand.

Thailand’s monetary policy is highly 
accommodative, with real interest 
rates falling despite a stable policy 
rate. We expect an extended policy 
rate pause at 1.00% in 2027 to 
support the uneven economy.

INFLATION AND MONETARY POLICY

### Page 7

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
7

Thailand’s current account was 
volatile and negative in the first 10 
months of 2026. Goods trade had a 
sizeable shortfall, and the services 
and income balances were in deficit.

External buffers will remain robust, 
despite falling import cover. This was 
driven mainly by surging imports, 
while foreign reserves have stayed 
resilient despite curbing FX volatility.

The decline in the goods trade 
balance reflected more than higher 
energy prices. It was also driven by 
higher capital goods imports from the 
technology-driven investment boom.

Portfolio flows have weakened and 
are volatile in 2H26 vs. strong inflows 
in 1H. Debt flows would be supported 
by stable domestic monetary policy, 
while equity flows stay volatile.

EXTERNAL POSITION

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026

## Document 390: The Big Picture_ Plunge Protection

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Big Picture_ Plunge Protection.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: The Big Picture: Plunge Protection
Date:    6 hours ago
The Big Picture: Plunge Protection

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
The Big Picture: Plunge
Protection
By Ed Yardeni • 23 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Ma Fo / Unsplash

I. Finding Support
The S&P 500 may be finding support around 7,000, which marked the
index's record high earlier this year, just before the Middle East war
started (chart). If so, the S&P 500 may have bottomed on Friday at
6,910.39. Yet another V-shaped rebound is also possible. Nevertheless,
we did lower our year-end target for the second month in a row on
Saturday to 7,500 from 7,900. We are still using $425 per share as our
2027 forward earnings estimate at year-end 2026. But we are lowering our

### Page 2

forward P/E estimate from 18.6 to 17.6 to reflect ongoing jitters about the
AI trade.
We still view the post-midterms selloff as a buying opportunity. We
currently project the S&P 500 will rise to 8,500 to 9,000 by the end of
2027.
We should mention that if support doesn't hold at 7,000, the next level is
the low of 6,343.72 on March 30 of this year.
The Nasdaq low on March 30, 2025 was 20,794.64.
II. Historical Perspective

### Page 3

We view the recent S&P 500 selloff as another correction in the bull
market that started on October 12, 2022 (chart).
The S&P 500 remains in a significant uptrend that began in March 2009,
marking the trough of the bear market of the Great Financial Crisis (chart).
The same holds for the Nasdaq (chart).

### Page 4

The Nasdaq has been outperforming the S&P 500 since the late 1970s
(chart).
III. Earnings
Analysts' consensus earnings estimates have been rising significantly for
2026, 2027, and 2028 since the start of this year (chart). This is a very
unusual trifecta. The previous trifecta was more muted in 2024, 2025, and
2026. The question is whether analysts' current projections are irrationally
exuberant. They might be, but we don't expect significant downward

revisions like those during the Tech Wreck of the early 2000s. Importantly,
we also don't expect a recession over the rest of the Roaring 2020s.

### Page 5

The strong upward revisions in analysts' earnings estimates for 2026-2028
are pushing S&P 500 forward earnings to record highs. That's likely to
continue if the rest of the decade remains recession-free. However, the
recent ascent in forward earnings is likely to slow, especially if industry
analysts cut their outlook for the volatile S&P 500 Semiconductor industry.
The y/y growth rate of S&P 500 forward earnings is highly correlated with
the comparable growth rate in nominal GDP (chart). The widespread

notion that the stock market is not the economy is wrong. The economy
obviously drives corporate earnings. Both are performing well.

### Page 6

IV. 2026 Versus Past 10 Years
For the S&P 500, 2026 has been much more volatile than the past 10-
year average (chart).
The same can be said for the Nasdaq (chart).

### Page 7

V. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E is now lower than it was on Liberation Day 2025
(chart). It is well below the peaks of the Tech Wreck in the early 2000s.
We don't see much more downside barring a recession.
The forward P/E of the S&P 500 Growth composite has plunged since
mid-2025 (chart).

### Page 8

Growth is very cheap. The ratio of the forward P/Es of S&P 500 Growth
and Value is down to 1.03, the lowest since the bear market of the Great
Financial Crisis (chart).

## Document 492: 一损俱损！如果AI泡沫破灭，对美国杀伤力超过“互联网泡沫”

Source PDF: all report pdf/一损俱损！如果AI泡沫破灭，对美国杀伤力超过“互联网泡沫”.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美国经济对AI⾼度依赖，AI投资贡献了去年经济增⻓的25%，股票占家庭净资产⽐重升⾄
35%，远超互联⽹泡沫顶峰。经济学家警告，⼀旦泡沫破裂，财富效应逆转叠加数据中⼼
债务⻛险，冲击可能超过2000年。更⿇烦的是，联邦债务已超GDP的100%，华盛顿的救
⽕空间远不如2008年。
摘要：
美国经济已经对AI⽀出上瘾。⼀旦这场繁荣熄⽕，冲击可能⽐互联⽹泡沫破裂更深。
据《华尔街⽇报》11⽉24⽇分析⽂章，过去两周股市剧烈震荡、英伟达董事会出现动荡，从硅
⾕到⽩宫都在追问同⼀个问题：如果AI繁荣破灭，美国经济会怎样？经济学家的共识是经济将遭
受痛苦打击，但对冲击量级仍存激烈分歧。
最直接的传导渠道是财富效应。⾃2025年初以来，标普500指数上涨约20%，其中AI相关股票贡
献了近⼀半涨幅。第三季度消费者⽀出增⻓3.8%，推动GDP增速达到3.4%。⼀旦股市掉头，这
条链条会迅速反向运转。
更棘⼿的是债务。数据中⼼的融资正变得越来越难追踪，许多贷款游离于资产负债表之外，由
私募股权基⾦等⾮银机构发放。哈佛⼤学经济学家、前美联储理事Jeremy Stein直⾔：“我们并

不了解所有的相互关联。”
美国经济对AI的依赖有多深
据巴克莱的⼀项估算，AI投资在过去⼀年贡献了美国经济增⻓的25%。⾃19世纪末铁路热潮以
来，还没有哪个全新产业的兴建在GDP中占据如此⼤的份额。
数据中⼼建设带动了建筑岗位，也让供应链上的制造商收⼊激增。⽣产发电机的卡特彼勒、涡
轮机制造商GE Vernova和电⽓设备公司伊顿，都报告了与数据中⼼热潮相关的收⼊⼤涨。暖通
空调制造商Trane Technologies⾸席执⾏官Dave Regnery在近期财报电话会上说：“我们热爱数
据中⼼。”
但这条增⻓引擎正⾯临政治和市场的双重压⼒。本⽉早些时候，⼀批⺠主党⼈当选国会议员，
部分竞选承诺就是遏制数据中⼼建设。如果这些承诺兑现，或者投资者对技术盈利前景失去信
⼼、超⼤规模云⼚商突然收缩建设，美国经济将失去⼀个关键动⼒。
不过，冲击或许没有想象中那么严重。巴克莱的Jonathan Millar指出，数据中⼼⽀出⼤部分⽤于
进⼝芯⽚，对国内GDP贡献有限。剔除这部分后，数据中⼼建设过去⼀年对GDP增⻓的拉动约

为0.5个百分点。
财富效应：股市下跌会直接打到消费
上⼀次股市崩盘引发美国衰退，还是互联⽹泡沫破裂。当时标普500指数腰斩，纳斯达克指数跌
去近80%，但经济下滑相对短暂，部分原因是失业率上升幅度⼩于其他衰退期。经济学家认
为，如果没有其他经济压⼒叠加，股市⼤跌往往只会带来消费的温和回落。
这⼀次的缓冲垫更薄。股票⽬前占家庭净资产的35%，⽽互联⽹泡沫顶峰时为26%。据美联储
数据，截⾄第⼆季度，最富有的1%家庭持有个⼈所持股票和共同基⾦份额的⼀半以上，⽽这些
富裕家庭正是消费⽀出的主要推⼿。⽤股票质押借款等⾼⻛险策略也很普遍。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
127 收藏 分享到:
⼀旦市场下跌，数以百万计的美国⼈会感到痛楚并收紧钱包。巴克莱⾼级美国经济学家
Jonathan Millar称，⽜市是“经济的⼀⼤顺⻛”，反过来，逆⻛同样强劲。
债务⻛险：更难追踪的“次贷式”隐忧
AI衰退会有多严重，可能取决于⾦融体系。房地产泡沫之所以演变成70多年来最严重的衰退，
关键在于问题蔓延⾄⾦融机构，信贷供给被掐断。如今⼀些经济学家开始看到2000年代中期次
级抵押贷款热潮与AI相关借贷之间的相似之处。
当年抵押贷款⽀持证券和衍⽣品构成的全球⽹络过于复杂，分析师难以理解其中的⻛险。眼
下，数据中⼼的借贷同样难以追踪：许多贷款不在资产负债表上，由报告要求更少的⾮银机构
发放；芯⽚制造商投资⾃⼰的客⼾，形成所谓的循环融资；各种交叉担保让外界更难厘清贷款
违约时谁最终暴露。

Stein说，虽然他认为类似2008年的危机不太可能，但他能想象经济受到的打击⽐互联⽹泡沫之
后更重的场景。“我敢打赌，经济后果会更⼤。”
好消息是，亚⻢逊和微软等超⼤规模云⼚商盈利能⼒强劲，迄今主要⽤⾃有现⾦⽽⾮债务⽀付
数据中⼼建设。哥伦⽐亚商学院经济学家van Nieuwerburgh估算，如果AI今天崩盘，数据中⼼
贷款损失可能只有500亿美元，在美国⾦融体系中微不⾜道。
坏消息是债务正在增⻓，并将在数据中⼼⽀出中占据越来越⼤的⽐重。van Nieuwerburgh预
计，如果借贷如预期般起⻜，两年后崩盘可能造成2万亿美元贷款损失，这还不包括股价下跌的
冲击。换句话说：如果你认为AI泡沫破裂不可避免，那你应该希望它明天就发⽣。
美国财政空间⽐2008年更窄
哈佛⼤学经济学家Jason Furman测算，即便股市估值下跌30%、数据中⼼建设完全停⽌，也不
⾜以引发衰退，因为数据中⼼的⼤量投⼊来⾃进⼝，其对GDP增⻓的贡献并没有普遍认为的那
么⼤。他最悲观的场景与互联⽹泡沫后的低迷相当。⽽在其他⼈看来，这已是最好的情形。

真正的问题在于，崩盘之后华盛顿可能⽆法像过去那样缓冲冲击。失业率飙升时，联邦政府通
常会打开资⾦闸⻔。但多年的⾼财政⾚字让这条路更难⾛。
美国公开持有的联邦债务最近突破了GDP的100%，⽽2008年⾦融危机前⼣仅为35%。低债务
⽔平曾让华盛顿得以通过向银⾏体系注资、维持⾼公共⽀出⾛出危机。这⼀次，回旋余地更
⼩。投资者对联邦债务的担忧，甚⾄可能在衰退期间推⾼美国国债收益率，让美联储更难通过
低利率刺激增⻓。Stein说：“这不是⼀个不合理的设想。”

中国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026 年 11 月 27 日 全 4 頁

中国：急発展する再生可能エネルギーと内巻 
太陽光・風力発電量を 25 年の 2.3 兆 kWh→30 年に 4 兆 kWh に拡大へ

調査本部 主席研究員 齋藤 尚登

[要約] 
 中国の太陽光発電と風力発電の発展が著しい。発電設備容量について、太陽光の構成
比は 2010 年の 0.0％から 2025 年は 30.8％に、同様に、風力発電は 3.1％から 16.4％
へと急拡大した。2010 年に 73.4％を占めていた火力発電のウエイトは徐々に低下し、
2025 年には 39.6％となった。一方、発電量の構成比は異なる様相を呈する。太陽光は
2010 年の 0.0％から 2025 年に 11.1％へ、風力は同様に 1.1％から 10.7％へと大きく拡
大したが、火力の 59.8％（2025 年）には遠く及ばない。太陽光発電や風力発電は気象
条件の影響を受け、稼働時間に制約があることがその背景だ。 
 2026 年 7 月に発表された「再生可能エネルギーの発展に関する第 15 次 5 カ年計画」で
は、2030 年までに再生可能エネルギーの発電設備容量を 35 億 kW（2025 年は 23.4 億
kW）へ拡大し、このうち太陽光発電と風力発電の設備容量を 28 億 kW（同 18.4 億 kW）
以上とし、非再生可能エネルギーを含む全体の発電設備容量に占める割合を 50％（同
47.3％）超に引き上げる、ことなどが目標に掲げられた。野心的な目標設定にみえる
が、これまでの爆発的な増加ぶりからすると、十分に達成可能であると思われる。 
 太陽光発電と風力発電はともに、過剰生産能力に伴う熾烈な価格競争に直面している。
いわゆる「内巻」の問題だ。結局のところ、問題の根源は過剰生産能力が増大する中
で、その削減が難しいことにある。国家の産業政策が奏功し、国有・民営企業を問わ
ず傾斜投資が行われ、国有商業銀行や地方の商業銀行が融資面で強力なサポートを提
供する。中央から見れば淘汰されてしかるべき企業・設備でも、地方にとっては保護
すべき対象ということになる。内巻の是正について、「再生可能エネルギーの発展に関
する第 15 次 5 カ年計画」でも抜本的な対策は盛り込まれていない。

太陽光発電・風力発電の発電設備容量が爆発的に増加 
中国の太陽光発電と風力発電の発展が著しい。発電設備容量を電源別に見たものが図表 1 で
あり、下段で構成比の推移を示している。これによると、太陽光発電は 2010 年の 0.0％から
2025 年は 30.8％に、同様に風力発電は 3.1％から 16.4％へと急拡大した。2010 年当時、73.4％
を占めていた火力発電のウエイトは徐々に低下し、2025 年には 39.6％となった。ちなみに、太
陽光発電と風力発電を合計すると、2025 年に初めて火力発電を上回り、2026 年 1 月～9 月は
10.2％pt の差を付けた。再生可能エネルギー（ここでは太陽光、風力、水力）の発電設備容量
は、2023 年に過半となり、2026 年 9 月には 6 割弱に達している。 
太陽光発電や風力発電の発電設備容量の爆発的な増加は、国家の産業政策の奏功によるとこ
ろが大きい。 
太陽光発電について、2009 年に中国財政部、科学技術部、国家エネルギー局が合同で「金太
陽パイロットプロジェクト」を実施したのを契機に、太陽光発電の導入が国家レベルで推進さ
れた。その後、2013 年に国務院が発表した「太陽光発電産業の健全な発展を促進することに関

する若干の意見」で、太陽光で発電された電力の固定価格買取制度が導入されたことが、太陽
光発電の発展に拍車をかけた。同制度は、地域ごとの日照条件に応じて、火力発電よりも高い
買取価格を設定して、太陽光発電のインセンティブとしたのである。このほか、太陽光発電関
連企業（ハイテク企業）に対する法人所得税の減免や各種補助金、銀行による低利融資の実施
などが、太陽光発電産業の勃興を支えた。

発電量の約 6 割は依然として火力発電。太陽光・風力は気象条件の影響が大 
次に、電源別の発電量を見たものが図表 2 であり、その構成比は、上記とは異なる様相を呈
している。太陽光発電の構成比は 2010 年の 0.0％から 2025 年に 11.1％へ、風力発電は同様に
1.1％から 10.7％へと大きく拡大しているが、火力発電の 59.8％（2025 年）には遠く及ばない。
太陽光発電や風力発電は気象条件の影響を受け、稼働時間に制約がある。当面の間、火力発電
が中国の主要電源である状況が続くとみられる。
